<li>In the seventeenth century, <a href="/wiki/Romani_people" title="Romani people">Romani people</a> were sent from the United Kingdom to work as slaves in the plantations in Barbados.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup></li></ol>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Barbados&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Languages">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>English is the <a href="/wiki/Official_language" title="Official language">official language</a> of Barbados, and is used for communications, administration, and public services all over the island. In its capacity as the official language of the country, the standard of English tends to conform to vocabulary, pronunciations, spellings, and conventions akin to, but not exactly the same as, those of <a href="/wiki/British_English" title="British English">British English</a>. For most people, however, <a href="/wiki/Bajan_Dialect" class="mw-redirect" title="Bajan Dialect">Bajan Dialect</a> is the language of everyday life. It does not have a standardised written form, but it is used by over 90% of the population.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Barbados&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Barbados" title="Religion in Barbados">Religion in Barbados</a></div>

<div class="thumbcaption">
<p>Religion in Barbados (2019)<sup id="cite_ref-2019religion_106-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2019religion-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r981673959">.mw-parser-output .legend{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .legend-color{display:inline-block;min-width:1.25em;height:1.25em;line-height:1.25;margin:1px 0;text-align:center;border:1px solid black;background-color:transparent;color:black}.mw-parser-output .legend-text{}</style><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:purple; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;Other Christian, including Baptist, Moravian, Mormon, and Jehovah's Witnesses (16.5%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:pink; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Seventh Day Adventist (5.9%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:salmon; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Methodist (4.2%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:Dodgerblue; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Roman Catholic (3.8%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:grey; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Wesleyans (3.4%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#89CFF0; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Church of God (2.4%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:teal; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;Nazarenes (3.2%)</div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"/><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:lightgrey; color:black;">&#160;</span>&#160;Other religion, including Muslim, Jewish, and Rastafarian (3%)</div>
</div>
</div></div>
<p>Christianity is the largest religion in Barbados, with the largest denomination being <a href="/wiki/Anglican_Communion" title="Anglican Communion">Anglican</a> (23.9% of the population in 2019).<sup id="cite_ref-2019religion_106-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2019religion-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> Other Christian denominations with significant followings in Barbados are the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a> (administered by <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Bridgetown" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Bridgetown">Roman Catholic Diocese of Bridgetown</a>), <a href="/wiki/Pentecostals" class="mw-redirect" title="Pentecostals">Pentecostals</a> (19.5%), <a href="/wiki/Jehovah%27s_Witness" class="mw-redirect" title="Jehovah&#39;s Witness">Jehovah's Witnesses</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Seventh-day_Adventist_Church" title="Seventh-day Adventist Church">Seventh-day Adventist Church</a> and <a href="/wiki/Spiritual_Baptist" title="Spiritual Baptist">Spiritual Baptists</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Encylopedia_Britannica-_Barbados_23-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Encylopedia_Britannica-_Barbados-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Church_of_England" title="Church of England">Church of England</a> was the official state religion until its legal disestablishment by the Parliament of Barbados following independence.<sup id="cite_ref-2019religion_106-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2019religion-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> As of 2019, 21% of Barbadians report having <a href="/wiki/Irreligion" title="Irreligion">no religion</a>, making the non-religious the second largest group after Anglicans.<sup id="cite_ref-FOTR_108-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOTR-108">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> Smaller religions in Barbados include <a href="/wiki/Hinduism" title="Hinduism">Hinduism</a>, <a href="/wiki/Islam_in_Barbados" title="Islam in Barbados">Islam</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith" title="Baháʼí Faith">Baháʼí Faith</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Barbados" title="History of the Jews in Barbados">Judaism</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Encylopedia_Britannica-_Barbados_23-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Encylopedia_Britannica-_Barbados-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The state is considered <a href="/wiki/Secular_state" title="Secular state">secular</a>, guaranteeing <a href="/wiki/Freedom_of_religion" title="Freedom of religion">freedom of religion or belief</a> to all and featuring only symbolic allusions to a higher power in the preamble to the constitution.<sup id="cite_ref-FOTR_108-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOTR-108">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Government_and_politics">Government and politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Barbados&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Government and politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Barbados" title="Government of Barbados">Government of Barbados</a> and <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Barbados" title="Politics of Barbados">Politics of Barbados</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Bridgetown_barbados_parliament_building.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/04/Bridgetown_barbados_parliament_building.jpg/220px-Bridgetown_barbados_parliament_building.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/04/Bridgetown_barbados_parliament_building.jpg/330px-Bridgetown_barbados_parliament_building.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/04/Bridgetown_barbados_parliament_building.jpg/440px-Bridgetown_barbados_parliament_building.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2272" data-file-height="1704" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Bridgetown_barbados_parliament_building.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Parliament_Buildings_(Barbados)" title="Parliament Buildings (Barbados)">Barbados parliament building</a> in Bridgetown</div></div></div>
<p>Barbados has been an independent country since 30 November 1966.<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> It functions as a <a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_republic" title="Parliamentary republic">parliamentary republic</a> modelled on the British <a href="/wiki/Westminster_system" title="Westminster system">Westminster system</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a> is the <a href="/wiki/President_of_Barbados" title="President of Barbados">President of Barbados</a> – presently <a href="/wiki/Sandra_Mason" title="Sandra Mason">Sandra Mason</a> – elected by the <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_Barbados" title="Parliament of Barbados">Parliament of Barbados</a> for a term of four years, and advised on matters of the Barbadian state by the <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Barbados" title="Prime Minister of Barbados">Prime Minister of Barbados</a>, who is <a href="/wiki/Head_of_government" title="Head of government">head of government</a>. There are 30 representatives within the <a href="/wiki/Barbados_House_of_Assembly" class="mw-redirect" title="Barbados House of Assembly">House of Assembly</a>, the lower chamber of Parliament. In the <a href="/wiki/Senate_of_Barbados" title="Senate of Barbados">Senate</a>, the upper chamber of Parliament, there are 21 senators.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Barbados" title="Constitution of Barbados">Constitution of Barbados</a> is the supreme law of the country.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> Legislation is passed by the Parliament of Barbados but does not have the force of law unless the President grants her assent to that law. The right to withhold assent is absolute and cannot be overridden by Parliament.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> The Attorney General heads <a href="/wiki/Judiciary_of_Barbados" title="Judiciary of Barbados">the independent judiciary</a>.
</p><p>During the 1990s, at the suggestion of <a href="/wiki/Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Trinidad and Tobago">Trinidad and Tobago</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Patrick_Manning" title="Patrick Manning">Patrick Manning</a>, Barbados attempted a <a href="/wiki/Political_union" title="Political union">political union</a> with Trinidad and Tobago and <a href="/wiki/Guyana" title="Guyana">Guyana</a>. The project stalled after the then prime minister of Barbados, <a href="/wiki/Lloyd_Erskine_Sandiford" title="Lloyd Erskine Sandiford">Lloyd Erskine Sandiford</a>, became ill and his <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Labour_Party_(Barbados)" title="Democratic Labour Party (Barbados)">Democratic Labour Party</a> lost the next general election.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> Barbados continues to share close ties with Trinidad and Tobago and with Guyana, claiming the highest number of Guyanese immigrants after the United States, Canada and the United Kingdom.
</p><p>Barbados is a party to the <a href="/wiki/Rome_Statute_of_the_International_Criminal_Court" class="mw-redirect" title="Rome Statute of the International Criminal Court">Rome Statute of the International Criminal Court</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Political_culture">Political culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Barbados&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Political culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Elections_in_Barbados" title="Elections in Barbados">Elections in Barbados</a></div>
<p>Barbados functions as a <a href="/wiki/Two-party_system" title="Two-party system">two-party system</a>. The dominant political parties are the <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Labour_Party_(Barbados)" title="Democratic Labour Party (Barbados)">Democratic Labour Party</a> and the incumbent <a href="/wiki/Barbados_Labour_Party" title="Barbados Labour Party">Barbados Labour Party</a>. Since independence on 30 November 1966, the Democratic Labour Party (DLP) has governed from 1966 to 1976; 1986 to 1994; and from 2008 to 2018; and the Barbados Labour Party (BLP) has governed from 1976 to 1986; 1994 to 2008; and from 2018 to present.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations">Foreign relations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Barbados&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Foreign relations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Barbados" title="Foreign relations of Barbados">Foreign relations of Barbados</a></div>
<p>Barbados follows a policy of <a href="/wiki/Non-Aligned_Movement" title="Non-Aligned Movement">nonalignment</a> and seeks cooperative relations with all friendly states. Barbados is a full and participating member of the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Community" title="Caribbean Community">Caribbean Community</a> (CARICOM), <a href="/wiki/CARICOM_Single_Market_and_Economy" title="CARICOM Single Market and Economy">CARICOM Single Market and Economy</a> (CSME), the <a href="/wiki/Association_of_Caribbean_States" title="Association of Caribbean States">Association of Caribbean States</a> (ACS),<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> the <a href="/wiki/Organization_of_American_States" title="Organization of American States">Organization of American States</a> (OAS), the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations" title="Commonwealth of Nations">Commonwealth of Nations</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Court_of_Justice" title="Caribbean Court of Justice">Caribbean Court of Justice</a> (CCJ). In 2005, Barbados replaced the <a href="/wiki/Judicial_Committee_of_the_Privy_Council" title="Judicial Committee of the Privy Council">Judicial Committee of the Privy Council</a> with the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Court_of_Justice" title="Caribbean Court of Justice">Caribbean Court of Justice</a> as its final court of appeal.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span id="World_Trade_Organization.2C_European_Commission.2C_CARIFORUM"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="World_Trade_Organization,_European_Commission,_CARIFORUM">World Trade Organization, European Commission, CARIFORUM</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Barbados&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: World Trade Organization, European Commission, CARIFORUM">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Barbados is an original member (1995) of the <a href="/wiki/World_Trade_Organization" title="World Trade Organization">World Trade Organization</a> (WTO) and participates actively in its work. It grants at least MFN treatment to all its trading partners. <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a> relations and cooperation with Barbados are carried out both on a bilateral and a regional basis. Barbados is party to the <a href="/wiki/Cotonou_Agreement" title="Cotonou Agreement">Cotonou Agreement</a>, through which, As of December&#160;2007<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Barbados&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, it is linked by an <a href="/wiki/Economic_Partnership_Agreement" class="mw-redirect" title="Economic Partnership Agreement">Economic Partnership Agreement</a> with the <a href="/wiki/European_Commission" title="European Commission">European Commission</a>. The pact involves the Caribbean Forum (CARIFORUM) subgroup of the <a href="/wiki/African,_Caribbean_and_Pacific_Group_of_States" class="mw-redirect" title="African, Caribbean and Pacific Group of States">African, Caribbean and Pacific Group of States</a> (ACP). CARIFORUM is the only part of the wider ACP-bloc that has concluded the full regional trade-pact with the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a>. There are also ongoing EU-<a href="/wiki/Community_of_Latin_American_and_Caribbean_States" title="Community of Latin American and Caribbean States">Community of Latin American and Caribbean States</a> (CELAC) and EU-<a href="/wiki/CARIFORUM" title="CARIFORUM">CARIFORUM</a> dialogues.<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Trade policy has also sought to protect a small number of domestic activities, mostly food production, from foreign competition, while recognising that most domestic needs are best met by imports.
</p>
<h4><span id="The_Double_Taxation_Relief_.28CARICOM.29_Treaty_1994"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="The_Double_Taxation_Relief_(CARICOM)_Treaty_1994">The Double Taxation Relief (CARICOM) Treaty 1994</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Barbados&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: The Double Taxation Relief (CARICOM) Treaty 1994">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>On 6 July 1994, at the Sherbourne Conference Centre, St. Michael, Barbados, representatives of eight countries signed the Double Taxation Relief (CARICOM) Treaties 1994. The countries which were represented were: Antigua and Barbuda, Belize, Grenada, Jamaica, St. Kitts and Nevis, St. Lucia, St. Vincent and the Grenadines, and Trinidad and Tobago.<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 19 August 1994, a representative of the Government of Guyana signed a similar treaty.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military_and_law_enforcement">Military and law enforcement</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Barbados&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Military and law enforcement">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Barbados_Defence_Force" title="Barbados Defence Force">Barbados Defence Force</a> has roughly 800 members. Within it, service members aged 14 to 18 years make up the Barbados Cadet Corps. The defence preparations of the island nation are closely tied to defence treaties with the United Kingdom, the United States, and the People's Republic of China.<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Barbados_Police_Service" title="Barbados Police Service">Barbados Police Service</a> is the sole law enforcement agency on the island of Barbados.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Barbados&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Administrative divisions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Parishes_of_Barbados" title="Parishes of Barbados">Parishes of Barbados</a></div>
<p>Barbados is <a href="/wiki/Administrative_division" title="Administrative division">divided</a> into 11 parishes:
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Barbados&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Barbados" title="Economy of Barbados">Economy of Barbados</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:312px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Barbados_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/Barbados_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/310px-Barbados_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="310" height="202" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/Barbados_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/465px-Barbados_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/Barbados_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/620px-Barbados_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Barbados_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A proportional representation of Barbados exports, 2019</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Barbados_electricity_production.svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/Barbados_electricity_production.svg/220px-Barbados_electricity_production.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="201" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/Barbados_electricity_production.svg/330px-Barbados_electricity_production.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/Barbados_electricity_production.svg/440px-Barbados_electricity_production.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="639" data-file-height="585" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Barbados_electricity_production.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Barbados electricity production by source</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:2022_Barbados_1_oz_Silver_Caribbean_Seahorse_reverse.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/2022_Barbados_1_oz_Silver_Caribbean_Seahorse_reverse.jpg/220px-2022_Barbados_1_oz_Silver_Caribbean_Seahorse_reverse.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="244" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/2022_Barbados_1_oz_Silver_Caribbean_Seahorse_reverse.jpg/330px-2022_Barbados_1_oz_Silver_Caribbean_Seahorse_reverse.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/15/2022_Barbados_1_oz_Silver_Caribbean_Seahorse_reverse.jpg/440px-2022_Barbados_1_oz_Silver_Caribbean_Seahorse_reverse.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1440" data-file-height="1594" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:2022_Barbados_1_oz_Silver_Caribbean_Seahorse_reverse.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>1 oz Silver Caribbean Seahorse - Sovereign coin backed by the Barbados government</div></div></div>
<p>Barbados is the 52nd <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_(nominal)_per_capita" title="List of countries by GDP (nominal) per capita">richest country in the world</a> in terms of GDP (Gross Domestic Product) per capita,<sup id="cite_ref-imf2_121-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-imf2-121">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> has a well-developed <a href="/wiki/Mixed_economy" title="Mixed economy">mixed economy</a>, and a moderately high <a href="/wiki/Standard_of_living" title="Standard of living">standard of living</a>. According to the World Bank, Barbados is one of 83 <a href="/wiki/High_income_economy" class="mw-redirect" title="High income economy">high income economies</a> in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> Despite this, a 2012 self-study in conjunction with the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Development_Bank" title="Caribbean Development Bank">Caribbean Development Bank</a> revealed 20% of Barbadians live in poverty, and nearly 10% cannot meet their basic daily food needs.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Historically, the economy of Barbados had been dependent on <a href="/wiki/Sugarcane" title="Sugarcane">sugarcane</a> cultivation and related activities, but since the late 1970s and early 1980s it has diversified into the manufacturing and tourism sectors.<sup id="cite_ref-Encylopedia_Britannica-_Barbados_23-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Encylopedia_Britannica-_Barbados-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> Offshore finance and information services have become important foreign exchange earners.
</p><p>Partly due to the staging of the 2007 Cricket World Cup, the island saw a construction boom, with the development and redevelopment of hotels, office complexes, and homes.<sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> This slowed during the 2008 to 2012 world economic crisis and the recession.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There was a strong economy between 1999 and 2000 but the economy went into recession in 2001 and 2002 due to slowdowns in tourism, consumer spending and the impact of the <a href="/wiki/11_September_2001_attacks" class="mw-redirect" title="11 September 2001 attacks">11 September 2001 attacks</a> in the United States and the <a href="/wiki/7_July_2005_London_bombings" title="7 July 2005 London bombings">7 July 2005 London bombings</a> in the United Kingdom. The economy rebounded in 2003 and has shown growth since 2004 which continued right through to 2008. The economy went into recession again from 2008 to 2013 before showing growth from 2014 to 2017. Then it declined to another recession from 2017 to 2019 during the world economic crisis. There were 23 downgrades by both Standard &amp; Poor's and Moody's in 2016, 2017 and 2018. The economy showed signs of recovery with 3 upgrades from Standard and Poor's and Moody's in 2019. From 1 January to 31 March 2020 the economy had started to grow, but then it experienced another decline due to the <a href="/wiki/COVID-19_recession" title="COVID-19 recession">COVID-19 economic recession</a>.
</p><p>Traditional trading partners include Canada, the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Community" title="Caribbean Community">Caribbean Community</a> (especially <a href="/wiki/Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Trinidad and Tobago">Trinidad and Tobago</a>), the United Kingdom and the United States. Recent government administrations have continued efforts to reduce unemployment, encourage <a href="/wiki/Foreign_direct_investment" title="Foreign direct investment">foreign direct investment</a>, and privatise remaining state-owned enterprises. Unemployment was reduced to 10.7% in 2003.<sup id="cite_ref-cia_1-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> However, it has since increased to 11.9% in second quarter, 2015.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The European Union is assisting Barbados with a €10 million program of modernisation of the country's International Business and Financial Services Sector.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Barbados maintains the third largest <a href="/wiki/Barbados_Stock_Exchange" title="Barbados Stock Exchange">stock exchange</a> in the Caribbean region. As of 2009<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Barbados&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, officials at the stock exchange were investigating the possibility of augmenting the local exchange with an International Securities Market (ISM) venture.<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sovereign_default_and_restructuring">Sovereign default and restructuring</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Barbados&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Sovereign default and restructuring">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>By May 2018, Barbados's outstanding debt climbed to <span style="white-space: nowrap">US$7.5 billion</span>, more than 1.7 times the country's <a href="/wiki/GDP" class="mw-redirect" title="GDP">GDP</a>. In June 2018 the government defaulted on its sovereign debt when it failed to make a coupon on <a href="/wiki/Eurobond_(international)" class="mw-redirect" title="Eurobond (international)">Eurobonds</a> maturing in 2035. Outstanding bond debt of Barbados reached <span style="white-space: nowrap">US$4.4 billion</span>.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In October 2019, Barbados concluded restructuring negotiations with a creditor group including investments funds <a href="/wiki/Eaton_Vance" title="Eaton Vance">Eaton Vance Management</a>, <a href="/wiki/Greylock_Capital_Management" title="Greylock Capital Management">Greylock Capital Management</a>, Teachers Advisors and Guyana Bank for Trade and Industry. Creditors will exchange existing bonds for a new debt series maturing in 2029. The new bonds involve a principal "haircut" of approximately 26% and include a clause allowing for deferment of principal and capitalization of interest in the event of a natural disaster.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Health">Health</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Barbados&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Health">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The main hospital on the island is the <a href="/wiki/Queen_Elizabeth_Hospital,_Bridgetown" title="Queen Elizabeth Hospital, Bridgetown">Queen Elizabeth Hospital</a>; however, Barbados has eight polyclinics across five parishes. There are also well-known medical care centres in Barbados such as Bayview Hospital, Sandy Crest Medical Centre and FMH Emergency Medical Clinic.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Barbados&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Education_in_Barbados" title="Education in Barbados">Education in Barbados</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Christ_Church,_Barbados_014.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e4/Christ_Church%2C_Barbados_014.jpg/170px-Christ_Church%2C_Barbados_014.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="227" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e4/Christ_Church%2C_Barbados_014.jpg/255px-Christ_Church%2C_Barbados_014.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e4/Christ_Church%2C_Barbados_014.jpg/340px-Christ_Church%2C_Barbados_014.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1944" data-file-height="2592" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Christ_Church,_Barbados_014.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Schoolchildren in <a href="/wiki/Saint_Philip,_Barbados" title="Saint Philip, Barbados">Saint Philip, Barbados</a></div></div></div>
<p>The Barbados <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_literacy_rate" title="List of countries by literacy rate">literacy rate</a> is ranked close to 100%.<sup id="cite_ref-unesco_132-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-unesco-132">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Encylopedia_Britannica-_Barbados_23-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Encylopedia_Britannica-_Barbados-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> The mainstream public education system of Barbados is fashioned after the British model. The government of Barbados spends 6.7% of its GDP on education (2008).<sup id="cite_ref-cia_1-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cia-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>All young people in the country must attend school until age 16. Barbados has over 70 primary schools and over 20 secondary schools throughout the island. There are a number of private schools, including those offering <a href="/wiki/Montessori_education" title="Montessori education">Montessori</a> and <a href="/wiki/International_Baccalaureate" title="International Baccalaureate">International Baccalaureate</a> education. Student enrolment at these schools represents less than 5% of the total enrolment of the public schools.
</p><p>Certificate-, diploma- and degree-level education in the country is provided by the <a href="/wiki/Barbados_Community_College" title="Barbados Community College">Barbados Community College</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Samuel_Jackman_Prescod_Institute_of_Technology" title="Samuel Jackman Prescod Institute of Technology">Samuel Jackman Prescod Institute of Technology</a>, <a href="/wiki/Codrington_College" title="Codrington College">Codrington College</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/University_of_the_West_Indies,_Cave_Hill_Campus" class="mw-redirect" title="University of the West Indies, Cave Hill Campus">Cave Hill campus</a> and Open Campus of the <a href="/wiki/University_of_the_West_Indies" title="University of the West Indies">University of the West Indies</a>. Barbados is also home to several overseas medical schools, such as <a href="/wiki/Ross_University_School_of_Medicine" title="Ross University School of Medicine">Ross University School of Medicine</a> and the <a href="/wiki/American_University_of_Integrative_Sciences" title="American University of Integrative Sciences">American University of Integrative Sciences, School of Medicine</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Educational_testing">Educational testing</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Barbados&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: Educational testing">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Barbados Secondary School Entrance Examination: Children who are 11 years old on 1 September in the year of the examination are required to write the examination as a means of allocation to secondary school.
</p><p>Caribbean Secondary Education Certificate (CSEC) examinations are usually taken by students after five years of secondary school and mark the end of standard secondary education. The CSEC examinations are equivalent to the Ordinary Level (O-Levels) examinations and are targeted toward students 16 and older.
</p><p>Caribbean Advanced Proficiency Examinations (CAPE) are taken by students who have completed their secondary education and wish to continue their studies. Students who sit for the CAPE usually possess CSEC or an equivalent certification. The CAPE is equivalent to the British Advanced Levels (A-Levels), voluntary qualifications that are intended for university entrance.<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Barbados&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Barbados" title="Culture of Barbados">Culture of Barbados</a></div>
<p>Barbados is a blend of West African, <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_culture" class="mw-redirect" title="Portuguese culture">Portuguese</a>, Creole, <a href="/wiki/Indian_culture" class="mw-redirect" title="Indian culture">Indian</a> and <a href="/wiki/British_culture" class="mw-redirect" title="British culture">British cultures</a>. Citizens are officially called Barbadians. The term "Bajan" (pronounced BAY-jun) may have come from a localised pronunciation of the word Barbadian, which at times can sound more like "Bar-bajan"; or, more likely, from English <i><a href="/wiki/Bay" title="Bay">bay</a></i> ("bayling"), Portuguese <span title="Portuguese-language text"><i lang="pt"><a href="https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/baiano" class="extiw" title="wikt:baiano">baiano</a></i></span>.
</p><p>The largest <a href="/wiki/Carnival" title="Carnival">carnival</a>-like cultural event that takes place on the island is the <a href="/wiki/Crop_Over" title="Crop Over">Crop Over</a> festival, which was established in 1974. As in many other Caribbean and Latin American countries, Crop Over is an important event for many people on the island, as well as the thousands of tourists that flock to there to participate in the annual events.<sup id="cite_ref-Encylopedia_Britannica-_Barbados_23-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Encylopedia_Britannica-_Barbados-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> The festival includes musical competitions and other traditional activities, and features the majority of the island's homegrown <a href="/wiki/Calypso_music" title="Calypso music">calypso</a> and <a href="/wiki/Soca_music" title="Soca music">soca music</a> for the year. The male and female Barbadians who harvested the most sugarcane are crowned as the King and Queen of the crop.<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> Crop Over gets under way at the beginning of July and ends with the costumed parade on Kadooment Day, held on the first Monday of August. New calypso/soca music is usually released and played more frequently from the beginning of May to coincide with the start of the festival.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cuisine">Cuisine</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Barbados&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: Cuisine">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Barbadian_cuisine" title="Barbadian cuisine">Barbadian cuisine</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Mount_Gay_Rum_Visitors_Centre,_Spring_Garden_HWY,_Barbados-002.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Mount_Gay_Rum_Visitors_Centre%2C_Spring_Garden_HWY%2C_Barbados-002.jpg/220px-Mount_Gay_Rum_Visitors_Centre%2C_Spring_Garden_HWY%2C_Barbados-002.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Mount_Gay_Rum_Visitors_Centre%2C_Spring_Garden_HWY%2C_Barbados-002.jpg/330px-Mount_Gay_Rum_Visitors_Centre%2C_Spring_Garden_HWY%2C_Barbados-002.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Mount_Gay_Rum_Visitors_Centre%2C_Spring_Garden_HWY%2C_Barbados-002.jpg/440px-Mount_Gay_Rum_Visitors_Centre%2C_Spring_Garden_HWY%2C_Barbados-002.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="1200" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Mount_Gay_Rum_Visitors_Centre,_Spring_Garden_HWY,_Barbados-002.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Mount_Gay_Rum" title="Mount Gay Rum">Mount Gay Rum</a> visitors centre</div></div></div>
<p>Bajan cuisine is a mixture of <a href="/wiki/Cuisine_of_Africa" class="mw-redirect" title="Cuisine of Africa">African</a>, <a href="/wiki/Indian_cuisine" title="Indian cuisine">Indian</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ireland" title="Ireland">Irish</a>, <a href="/wiki/Creole_peoples" title="Creole peoples">Creole</a> and <a href="/wiki/British_cuisine" title="British cuisine">British</a> influences. A typical meal consists of a main dish of meat or fish, normally marinated with a mixture of herbs and spices, hot side dishes, and one or more salads. A common Bajan side dish could be pickled cucumber, fish cakes, bake, etc. The meal is usually served with one or more sauces.<sup id="cite_ref-Totally_135-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Totally-135">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/National_dish" title="National dish">national dish</a> of Barbados is <a href="/wiki/Cou-cou" title="Cou-cou">cou-cou</a> and <a href="/wiki/Flying_fish" title="Flying fish">flying fish</a> with spicy gravy.<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> Another traditional meal is pudding and souse, a dish of pickled <a href="/wiki/Pork" title="Pork">pork</a> with spiced sweet potatoes.<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> A wide variety of seafood and meats are also available.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Mount_Gay_Rum" title="Mount Gay Rum">Mount Gay Rum</a> visitor's centre in Barbados claims to be the world's oldest remaining rum company, with the earliest confirmed deed from 1703. <a href="/wiki/Cockspur_Rum" title="Cockspur Rum">Cockspur Rum</a> and <a href="/wiki/Malibu_(rum)" title="Malibu (rum)">Malibu</a> are also from the island. Barbados is home to the <a href="/wiki/Banks_Barbados_Brewery" title="Banks Barbados Brewery">Banks Barbados Brewery</a>, which brews Banks Beer, a <a href="/wiki/Pale_lager" title="Pale lager">pale lager</a>, as well as Banks <a href="/wiki/Amber_Ale" class="mw-redirect" title="Amber Ale">Amber Ale</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> Banks also brews Tiger Malt, a non-alcoholic <a href="/wiki/Malta" title="Malta">malted beverage</a>. 10 Saints beer is brewed in <a href="/wiki/Speightstown" title="Speightstown">Speightstown</a>, St. Peter in Barbados and aged for 90 days in Mount Gay 'Special Reserve' Rum casks. It was first brewed in 2009 and is available in certain Caricom nations.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Music">Music</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Barbados&amp;action=edit&amp;section=37" title="Edit section: Music">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Barbados" title="Music of Barbados">Music of Barbados</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Rihanna-brisbane.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Rihanna-brisbane.jpg/170px-Rihanna-brisbane.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="227" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Rihanna-brisbane.jpg/255px-Rihanna-brisbane.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Rihanna-brisbane.jpg/340px-Rihanna-brisbane.jpg 2x" data-file-width="768" data-file-height="1024" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Rihanna-brisbane.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>International <a href="/wiki/Celebrity" title="Celebrity">pop star</a> <a href="/wiki/Rihanna" title="Rihanna">Rihanna</a>, a native of Barbados, is a <a href="/wiki/Grammy_awards" class="mw-redirect" title="Grammy awards">nine-time Grammy Award winner</a> and one of the <a href="/wiki/Best_selling_music_artists" class="mw-redirect" title="Best selling music artists">best selling music artists</a> of all time, selling over 200&#160;million records worldwide. In 2009 she was appointed as an Honorary Ambassador of Youth and Culture for Barbados by the late Prime Minister, <a href="/wiki/David_Thompson_(Barbadian_politician)" title="David Thompson (Barbadian politician)">David Thompson</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup></div></div></div>
<div class="excerpt-block"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1066933788">.mw-parser-output .excerpt-hat .mw-editsection-like{font-style:normal}</style><div class="excerpt">
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Barbados" title="Music of Barbados">music of Barbados</a> includes distinctive national styles of <a href="/wiki/Folk_music" title="Folk music">folk</a> and <a href="/wiki/Popular_music" title="Popular music">popular music</a>, including elements of <a href="/wiki/Western_classical_music" class="mw-redirect" title="Western classical music">Western classical</a> and <a href="/wiki/Religious_music" title="Religious music">religious music</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Barbados" title="Culture of Barbados">culture of Barbados</a> is a syncretic mix of African and British elements, and the island's music reflects this mix through song types and styles, instrumentation, dances, and aesthetic principles.
</p>
Barbadian folk traditions include the <a href="/wiki/Landship_(Barbados)" class="mw-redirect" title="Landship (Barbados)">Landship</a> movement, which is a satirical, informal organization based on the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Navy" title="Royal Navy">Royal Navy</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tea_meeting" title="Tea meeting">tea meetings</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tuk_band" title="Tuk band">tuk bands</a> and numerous traditional songs and dances. In modern Barbados, popular styles include <a href="/wiki/Calypso_music" title="Calypso music">calypso</a>, <a href="/wiki/Spouge" title="Spouge">spouge</a>, <a href="/wiki/Contemporary_folk" class="mw-redirect" title="Contemporary folk">contemporary folk</a> and <a href="/wiki/World_music" title="World music">world music</a>. Barbados is, along with Guadeloupe, Martinique, Trinidad, Cuba, Puerto Rico, and the Virgin Islands, one of the few centres for Caribbean jazz.</div></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports">Sports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Barbados&amp;action=edit&amp;section=38" title="Edit section: Sports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Sport_in_Barbados" title="Sport in Barbados">Sport in Barbados</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Kensington_Oval_yes.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/38/Kensington_Oval_yes.jpg/220px-Kensington_Oval_yes.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/38/Kensington_Oval_yes.jpg/330px-Kensington_Oval_yes.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/38/Kensington_Oval_yes.jpg/440px-Kensington_Oval_yes.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="600" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Kensington_Oval_yes.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Kensington_Oval" title="Kensington Oval">Kensington Oval</a> in <a href="/wiki/Bridgetown" title="Bridgetown">Bridgetown</a> hosted the <a href="/wiki/2007_Cricket_World_Cup_Final" title="2007 Cricket World Cup Final">2007 Cricket World Cup final</a>. Cricket is one of the most followed games in Barbados and Kensington Oval is often referred to as the "Mecca in Cricket" due to its significance and contributions to the sport.</div></div></div> <div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Horesandjockey.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/82/Horesandjockey.jpg/220px-Horesandjockey.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="157" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/82/Horesandjockey.jpg/330px-Horesandjockey.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/82/Horesandjockey.jpg/440px-Horesandjockey.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2218" data-file-height="1584" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Horesandjockey.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A horse and rider at Garrison Savannah</div></div></div>
<p>As in other Caribbean countries of British colonial heritage, <a href="/wiki/Cricket" title="Cricket">cricket</a> is very popular on the island. The <a href="/wiki/West_Indies_cricket_team" title="West Indies cricket team">West Indies cricket team</a> usually includes several Barbadian players. In addition to several warm-up matches and six "Super Eight" matches, the country hosted the final of the <a href="/wiki/2007_Cricket_World_Cup" title="2007 Cricket World Cup">2007 Cricket World Cup</a>. Barbados has produced many great cricketers including <a href="/wiki/Sir_Garfield_Sobers" class="mw-redirect" title="Sir Garfield Sobers">Sir Garfield Sobers</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sir_Frank_Worrell" class="mw-redirect" title="Sir Frank Worrell">Sir Frank Worrell</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sir_Clyde_Walcott" class="mw-redirect" title="Sir Clyde Walcott">Sir Clyde Walcott</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sir_Everton_Weekes" class="mw-redirect" title="Sir Everton Weekes">Sir Everton Weekes</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gordon_Greenidge" title="Gordon Greenidge">Gordon Greenidge</a>, <a href="/wiki/Wes_Hall" title="Wes Hall">Wes Hall</a>, <a href="/wiki/Charlie_Griffith" title="Charlie Griffith">Charlie Griffith</a>, <a href="/wiki/Joel_Garner" title="Joel Garner">Joel Garner</a>, <a href="/wiki/Desmond_Haynes" title="Desmond Haynes">Desmond Haynes</a> and <a href="/wiki/Malcolm_Marshall" title="Malcolm Marshall">Malcolm Marshall</a>.
</p><p>In <a href="/wiki/Track_and_Field" class="mw-redirect" title="Track and Field">Track and Field</a> <a href="/wiki/Obadele_Thompson" title="Obadele Thompson">Obadele Thompson</a> won a bronze medal in the <a href="/wiki/100m" class="mw-redirect" title="100m">100m</a> at the <a href="/wiki/2000_Summer_Olympic_Games" class="mw-redirect" title="2000 Summer Olympic Games">2000 Summer Olympic Games</a> He's the first to win an Olympic medal, Other Olympic medallists include <a href="/wiki/Shaunae_Miller-Uibo" title="Shaunae Miller-Uibo">Shaunae Miller-Uibo</a>. He is also the only Bajan to run sub 10 and sub 20 over 100m and <a href="/wiki/200m" class="mw-redirect" title="200m">200m</a>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Ryan_Brathwaite" title="Ryan Brathwaite">Ryan Brathwaite</a><sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup> won a <a href="/wiki/Gold_medal" title="Gold medal">gold medal</a> in the <a href="/wiki/110_metres_hurdles" title="110 metres hurdles">110 metres hurdles</a> at the <a href="/wiki/2009_World_Championships_in_Athletics" title="2009 World Championships in Athletics">2009 World Championships in Athletics</a> in Berlin.<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Rugby_union" title="Rugby union">Rugby</a> is also popular in Barbados.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Horse_racing" title="Horse racing">Horse racing</a> takes place at the <a href="/wiki/Garrison_Savannah_Racetrack" title="Garrison Savannah Racetrack">Historic Garrison Savannah</a> close to Bridgetown. Spectators can pay for admission to the stands, or else can watch races from the public "rail", which encompasses the track.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Basketball" title="Basketball">Basketball</a> is an increasingly popular sport, played at school or college. <a href="/wiki/Barbados_national_basketball_team" class="mw-redirect" title="Barbados national basketball team">Barbados's national team</a> has shown some unexpected results as in the past it beat many much larger countries.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Polo" title="Polo">Polo</a> is very popular amongst the rich elite on the island and the "High-Goal" Apes Hill team is based at the St James's Club.<sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> It is also played at the private <a href="/wiki/Holders_Festival" class="mw-redirect" title="Holders Festival">Holders Festival</a> ground.
</p><p>In golf, the <a href="/wiki/Barbados_Open" class="mw-redirect" title="Barbados Open">Barbados Open</a>, played at Royal Westmoreland Golf Club, was an annual stop on the <a href="/wiki/European_Seniors_Tour" class="mw-redirect" title="European Seniors Tour">European Seniors Tour</a> from 2000 to 2009. In December 2006 the <a href="/wiki/WGC-World_Cup" class="mw-redirect" title="WGC-World Cup">WGC-World Cup</a> took place at the country's <a href="/wiki/Sandy_Lane_(resort)" title="Sandy Lane (resort)">Sandy Lane</a> resort on the Country Club course, an 18-hole course designed by <a href="/wiki/Tom_Fazio" title="Tom Fazio">Tom Fazio</a>. The Barbados Golf Club is another course on the island. It has hosted the Barbados Open on several occasions.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Volleyball" title="Volleyball">Volleyball</a> is also popular and is mainly played indoors.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Tennis" title="Tennis">Tennis</a> is gaining popularity and Barbados is home to <a href="/wiki/Darian_King" title="Darian King">Darian King</a>, who has achieved a career-high ranking of 106 in May 2017 and has played in the <a href="/wiki/Tennis_at_the_2016_Summer_Olympics_%E2%80%93_Men%27s_singles" title="Tennis at the 2016 Summer Olympics – Men&#39;s singles">2016 Summer Olympics</a> and the <a href="/wiki/2017_US_Open_%E2%80%93_Men%27s_Singles" class="mw-redirect" title="2017 US Open – Men&#39;s Singles">2017 US Open</a>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Motorsports" class="mw-redirect" title="Motorsports">Motorsports</a> also play a role, with Rally Barbados occurring each summer and being listed on the FIA NACAM calendar. Also, the <a href="/wiki/Bushy_Park,_Barbados" class="mw-redirect" title="Bushy Park, Barbados">Bushy Park Circuit</a> hosted the <a href="/wiki/Race_of_Champions" title="Race of Champions">Race of Champions</a> and <a href="/wiki/Global_RallyCross_Championship" class="mw-redirect" title="Global RallyCross Championship">Global RallyCross Championship</a> in 2014.
</p><p>The presence of the trade winds along with favourable swells make the southern tip of the island an ideal location for wave sailing (an extreme form of the sport of <a href="/wiki/Windsurfing" title="Windsurfing">windsurfing</a>).
</p><p>Barbados also hosts several international surfing competitions.<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Netball" title="Netball">Netball</a> is also popular with women in Barbados.
</p><p>Several players in the <a href="/wiki/National_Football_League" title="National Football League">National Football League</a> (NFL) are from Barbados, including <a href="/wiki/Robert_Bailey_(American_football)" title="Robert Bailey (American football)">Robert Bailey</a>, <a href="/wiki/Roger_Farmer" title="Roger Farmer">Roger Farmer</a>, <a href="/wiki/Elvis_Joseph" title="Elvis Joseph">Elvis Joseph</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Sam_Seale" title="Sam Seale">Sam Seale</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport">Transport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Barbados&amp;action=edit&amp;section=39" title="Edit section: Transport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_Barbados" title="Transport in Barbados">Transport in Barbados</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Barbados_Midibus1.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/Barbados_Midibus1.jpg/220px-Barbados_Midibus1.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="172" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/Barbados_Midibus1.jpg/330px-Barbados_Midibus1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2f/Barbados_Midibus1.jpg/440px-Barbados_Midibus1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2764" data-file-height="2160" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Barbados_Midibus1.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A Hino ACME Minibus B 163 in Speightstown, St. Peter, Barbados</div></div></div>
<p>Although Barbados is about 34&#160;km (21&#160;mi) across at its widest point, a car journey from Six Cross Roads in St. Philip (south-east) to North Point in St. Lucy (north-central) can take one and a half hours or longer due to traffic. Barbados has half as many registered cars as citizens. In Barbados, drivers drive on the left side of the road.
</p><p>Barbados is known for its many roundabouts. One famous roundabout is a roundabout located east of Bridgetown, where you will see an emancipation statue commonly associated with a slave named <a href="/wiki/Bussa%27s_rebellion" title="Bussa&#39;s rebellion">Bussa</a>.
</p><p>Transport on the island is relatively convenient with "<a href="/wiki/Share_taxis" class="mw-redirect" title="Share taxis">route taxis</a>" called "<a href="/wiki/ZR_(bus)" title="ZR (bus)">ZRs</a>" (pronounced "Zed-Rs") travelling to most points on the island. These small buses can at times be crowded, as passengers are generally never turned down regardless of the number. They will usually take the more scenic routes to destinations. They generally depart from the capital <a href="/wiki/Bridgetown" title="Bridgetown">Bridgetown</a> or from <a href="/wiki/Speightstown,_Barbados" class="mw-redirect" title="Speightstown, Barbados">Speightstown</a> in the northern part of the island.
</p><p>Including the ZRs, there are three bus systems running seven days a week (though less frequently on Sundays). There are ZRs, the yellow minibuses and the blue <a href="/wiki/Barbados_Transport_Board" title="Barbados Transport Board">Transport Board buses</a>. A ride on any of them costs Bds$3.5.<sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> The smaller buses from the two privately owned systems ("ZRs" and "minibuses") can give change; the larger blue buses from the government-operated <a href="/wiki/Barbados_Transport_Board" title="Barbados Transport Board">Barbados Transport Board</a> system cannot, but do give receipts. The Barbados Transport Board buses travel in regular bus routes and scheduled timetables across Barbados. Schoolchildren in school uniform including some Secondary schools ride for free on the government buses and for Bds$2.5 on the ZRs. Most routes require a connection in Bridgetown. Barbados Transport Board's headquarters are located at Kay's House, Roebuck Street, St. Michael, and the bus depots and terminals are located in the Fairchild Street Bus Terminal in Fairchild Street and the Princess Alice Bus Terminal (which was formerly the Lower Green Bus Terminal in Jubilee Gardens, Bridgetown, St. Michael) in Princess Alice Highway, Bridgetown, St. Michael; the Speightstown Bus Terminal in Speightstown, St. Peter; the Oistins Bus Depot in Oistins, Christ Church; and the Mangrove Bus Depot in Mangrove, St. Philip. In July 2020, the <a href="/wiki/Barbados_Transport_Board" title="Barbados Transport Board">Barbados Transport Board</a> received 33 <a href="/wiki/BYD_Auto" title="BYD Auto">BYD</a> electric buses which were obtained not only to add to the aging fleet of diesel buses but also to assist the Government in their goal of eliminating the use of fossil fuels by 2030.<sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Some hotels also provide visitors with shuttles to points of interest on the island from outside the hotel lobby. There are several locally owned and operated vehicle rental agencies in Barbados but there are no multi-national companies.
</p><p>The island's lone airport is the <a href="/wiki/Grantley_Adams_International_Airport" title="Grantley Adams International Airport">Grantley Adams International Airport</a>. It receives daily flights by several major airlines from points around the globe, as well as several smaller regional commercial airlines and charters. The airport serves as the main air-transportation hub for the eastern Caribbean. In the first decade of the 21st century it underwent a <span style="white-space: nowrap">US$100 million</span> upgrade and expansion in February 2003 until completion in August 2005.
</p><p>The island also has a sea port which is the primary port of call for commercial container and cruise traffic.
</p><p>There was also a helicopter shuttle service, which offered air taxi services to a number of sites around the island, mainly on the West Coast tourist belt. Air and maritime traffic was regulated by the <a href="/wiki/Barbados_Port_Authority" class="mw-redirect" title="Barbados Port Authority">Barbados Port Authority</a>.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="See_also">See also</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Barbados&amp;action=edit&amp;section=40" title="Edit section: See also">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

As an aside, the word "<i>Ichirouganaim</i>", said to be an Arawak word used by the Amerindians to describe Barbados, is thought to refer to the "teeth" imagery of the waves breaking on the reefs off most of southern and eastern coasts.</q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Ajournal&amp;rft.genre=unknown&amp;rft.jtitle=Guyana+Chronicle&amp;rft.atitle=Guyana+Consul+%28Barbados%29+Visit+to+Former+Amerindian+Village+Site+in+B%27dos&amp;rft.pages=2&amp;rft.date=2009-06-17&amp;rft.aulast=Faria&amp;rft.aufirst=Norman&amp;rft_id=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.pantribalconfederacy.com%2Fconfederacy%2FNews%2Fpdf%2Fguyana_consul_visit.pdf&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3ABarbados" class="Z3988"></span></span>

<li id="cite_note-62"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-62">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite id="CITEREFMadden2020" class="citation news cs1">Madden, Marlon, ed. (17 September 2020). <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://barbadostoday.bb/2020/09/17/wickham-predicts-barbados-republic-model-to-mirror-trinidads/">"Wickham predicts Barbados' republic model to mirror Trinidad's"</a>. Top Featured Article. Retrieved <span class="nowrap">4 June</span> 2021</span>. <q>As Barbados prepares to ditch the Queen as its Head of State and become a republic, a prominent political scientist is predicting that Prime Minister Mia Mottley will follow the Trinidad and Tobago model. What's more, Peter Wickham has shot down any idea of the Barbados Labour Party administration holding a referendum on the matter, saying that to do so would be a 'mistake'. 'There is no need to and I don't think it makes a lot of sense. We had a situation where since 1999 this [political party] indicated its desire to go in the direction of a republic. The Opposition has always supported it ... So, I think there is enough cohesion in that regard to go with it,' he said.</q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Ajournal&amp;rft.genre=article&amp;rft.atitle=Wickham+predicts+Barbados%27+republic+model+to+mirror+Trinidad%27s&amp;rft.date=2020-09-17&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fbarbadostoday.bb%2F2020%2F09%2F17%2Fwickham-predicts-barbados-republic-model-to-mirror-trinidads%2F&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3ABarbados" class="Z3988"></span></span>

<li id="cite_note-73"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-73">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite id="CITEREFLogan" class="citation news cs1">Logan, Gabi. <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://traveltips.usatoday.com/geologic-history-barbados-beaches-57660.html">"Geologic History of Barbados Beaches"</a>. <i><a href="/wiki/USA_Today" title="USA Today">USA Today</a></i>. <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20120322063852/http://traveltips.usatoday.com/geologic-history-barbados-beaches-57660.html">Archived</a> from the original on 22 March 2012<span class="reference-accessdate">. Retrieved <span class="nowrap">2 July</span> 2011</span>. <q>Barbados lies directly over the intersection of the Caribbean plate and the South American plate in a region known as a subduction zone. Beneath the ocean floor, the South American plate slowly slides below the Caribbean plate.</q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Ajournal&amp;rft.genre=article&amp;rft.jtitle=USA+Today&amp;rft.atitle=Geologic+History+of+Barbados+Beaches&amp;rft.aulast=Logan&amp;rft.aufirst=Gabi&amp;rft_id=http%3A%2F%2Ftraveltips.usatoday.com%2Fgeologic-history-barbados-beaches-57660.html&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3ABarbados" class="Z3988"></span></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-74"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-74">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite class="citation web cs1"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.barbadosbeachhouse.com/barbados_sightseeing_animal_flower_cave.php">"Barbados Sightseeing – Animal Flower Cave"</a>. <i>Leigh Designs</i>. Little Bay House. 2010. <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20111212233710/http://www.barbadosbeachhouse.com/barbados_sightseeing_animal_flower_cave.php">Archived</a> from the original on 12 December 2011<span class="reference-accessdate">. Retrieved <span class="nowrap">10 July</span> 2011</span>. <q>The Animal flower Cave is the island's lone accessible sea-cave and was discovered from the sea in 1780 by two English explorers. The cave's coral floor is estimated to be 400,000 to 500,000 years old and the "younger" coral section above the floor is about 126,000 years old. The dating was carried out by the German Geographical Institute, and visitors can see a "map" of the dating work in the bar and restaurant. The cave now stands some six feet above the high tide mark even though it was formed at sea level. This is because Barbados is rising about one inch per 1,000 years, which is yet another indication of the cave's age.</q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Ajournal&amp;rft.genre=unknown&amp;rft.jtitle=Leigh+Designs&amp;rft.atitle=Barbados+Sightseeing+%E2%80%93+Animal+Flower+Cave&amp;rft.date=2010&amp;rft_id=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.barbadosbeachhouse.com%2Fbarbados_sightseeing_animal_flower_cave.php&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3ABarbados" class="Z3988"></span></span>
</li>

Retrieved <span class="nowrap">3 April</span> 2017</span>.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Ajournal&amp;rft.genre=unknown&amp;rft.jtitle=barbadospocketguide.com&amp;rft.atitle=Pico+Teneriffe+%E2%80%93+Barbados+Pocket+Guide&amp;rft.au=Gloria&amp;rft_id=http%3A%2F%2Fwww.barbadospocketguide.com%2Fbarbados-attractions%2Fbeaches-and-bays%2Fnorth-coast-beaches%2Fpico-teneriffe.html&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3ABarbados" class="Z3988"></span></span>

</div></td></tr><tr><td class="navbox-abovebelow" colspan="2"><div><b>N.B.:</b> Territories in <i>italics</i> are parts of transregional sovereign states or non-sovereign dependencies.
<p><sup>^</sup>These three form the <a href="/wiki/SSS_islands" title="SSS islands">SSS islands</a> that with the ABC islands comprise the <a href="/wiki/Dutch_Caribbean" title="Dutch Caribbean">Dutch Caribbean</a>, of which <sup>*</sup>the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Netherlands" title="Caribbean Netherlands">BES islands</a> are not direct <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_the_Netherlands" title="Kingdom of the Netherlands">Kingdom</a> constituents but subsumed with the country of the <a href="/wiki/Netherlands" title="Netherlands">Netherlands</a>.
</p><p><sup>†</sup><a href="/wiki/Physical_geography" title="Physical geography">Physiographically</a>, these <a href="/wiki/Island#Continental_islands" title="Island">continental islands</a> are not part of the volcanic Windward Islands arc, although sometimes grouped with them culturally and politically.
</p><p><sup>ǂ</sup><a href="/wiki/Territorial_dispute" title="Territorial dispute">Disputed territories</a> administered by <a href="/wiki/Guyana" title="Guyana">Guyana</a>. <sup>~</sup><a href="/wiki/Territorial_dispute" title="Territorial dispute">Disputed territories</a> administered by <a href="/wiki/Colombia" title="Colombia">Colombia</a>.
</p>
<sup>#</sup><a href="/wiki/Bermuda" title="Bermuda">Bermuda</a> is an isolated <a href="/wiki/Atlantic_Ocean" title="Atlantic Ocean">North Atlantic</a> <a href="/wiki/Island#Oceanic_islands" title="Island">oceanic island</a>, physiographically not part of the Lucayan Archipelago, Antilles, Caribbean Sea nor North American continental nor South American continental islands. It is grouped with the <a href="/wiki/North_America" title="North America">Northern American</a> region, but occasionally also with the Caribbean region culturally.</div></td></tr></tbody></table></div>
